=== PDF PAGE 3 · Employee alarm system ===
JSC Employee
Alarm System
The JSC site-wide emergency alerting system
is used to quickly notify employees and visi-
tors of potential danger. In the event of an
emergency, the following tones will be used,
and people should respond as indicated.
мHODH WHOOO WHOOD
Whoop (low to high siren) - Seek shelter and get more
information
3-5 Minute Wavering Tone - Attack Warning
(nuclear or conventional)
(air raid warning)
Wail (single, constant tone) - All Clear
Short Wail (like a noon whistle) - Periodic System Test
(1st Thursday of
every month at noon)
When the siren sounds, take appropriate
immediate protective actions. If you are
already indoors, alert others who may not
have heard the sound to prevent them from
going outside. Close doors and windows, and
notify the Facility Manager, Safety Officer, or
supervisor. Call the emergency number
(X33333) if you have critical information to pro-
vide. JSC Television and the Emergency
Intormation Line (483-3351) will provide addi-
tional information as it becomes available.

JSC Emergency
Warning (EWS)
Policy & Procedures
• The JSC Emergency Warning System alert
tones will be used only when employees
are in imminent danger and must take
immediate action for their protection
• EOC operation procedures will include
instructions for immediate activation of the
employee alarm system when employees
are immediately threatened by a chemical
release or tornado in close proximity to
JSC.
• The JSC Incident Commander is author-
ized to activate the employee alarm system
during emergency conditions.
• Any other use of the employee alarm
systems requires approval of the Center
Operation Director or higher authority.
• Warning tones and periodic testing have
been coordinated with neighboring commu-
nities and the Bay Area Local Emergency
Planning Committee.
• A monthly systems test will be conducted
on the first Thursday of each month at
noon. Neighboring communities also test
the sirens on the first Thursday but at
dinner time.
• Employees, neighboring communities, fire
and police departments, and broadcast
media will be intormed of the periodic test
schedule and will be notified whenever the
siren system is used for an emergency.
• Facility Managers will be notified whenever
the siren system is not operational.
OPERATIONA  PEMATONECEATEE
Armnettn 2001

=== PDF PAGE 4 · Introduction ===
x.sias.x
The JSC Telephone Directory
Introduction
What's New?
As most users who have access to the JSC Home Page know,
the most current directory information is available online from two
sources: the Global Address List (GAL) which provides the
directory information as part of the e-mail address of an individual, or
through the online directory available at http://www3.jsc.nasa.gov/cgi-
bin/prod/phone/phone.cgi. The Online Directory includes a wealth of
information such as the current JSC Organization Listing, lists of facility
managers, conference rooms, off-site contractors and links to other
directory services.
Recognizing that the need still exists for a hard copy version of the JSC
Telephone Directory, the Information Systems Directorate is making a
compromise. This version of the directory represents the LAST one of its
kind-a completely new printed version made available to directorate,
division and off-site contractor offices.
Where Does It Go From Here?
From this point forward, sections of the book will be made available on the
Online Directory Web site as they are updated. Needed sections may be
printed at your local printer. Organizations with valid requirements for hard
copy versions may still request them by contacting the JSC Print Shop at
281.483.6156 or the ISD Customer Support Office at 281.483.4716.
What's Included?
The Online Directory includes most of the traditional sections that you're
accustomed to seeing. See the Table of Contents for a full listing. To
ensure that only the most current information is available in those areas
used primarily by onsite residents, the Telephone Directory no longer
includes printed versions of the list of facility managers, conference
rooms, the blood drive schedule, etc. Instead, page 3 contains the
URLs for these lists.
Quick Tips:
• For the most current information, use the Telephone
Directory link at the top of the JSC Home Page at
http://www4.jsc.nasa.gov/orgo directly to
http://www3.jsc.nasa.gov/cgi-bin/prod/phone/phone.cgi.
• Any questions? Contact the ISD Customer Support
Office at 281.483.4716 or drop us an e-mail: ISD
Customer Support

=== PDF PAGE 8 · General information ===
General Information
General
Telephones installed for Government use are for
OFFICIAL business only.
The Information Systems Directorate (GA) and Information
Technology Division (GT) / Communications Branch,
through its contractors, furnishes all equipment necessary
to provide telephone service. In the interest of safety and
good service, no equipment, apparatus, or device not
provided or approved by the Information Technology
Division may be attached to, used or connected with JSC
cable plant or other facilities. Under no circumstances shall
telephone users move, add equipment to. alter, rewire
jacks for, or attempt repair of telephones or associated
equipment.
JSC Calls
All telephones at JSC (including Ellington Field and
Sonny Carter Training Facility) are accessed using 5-digit
dialing.
Local Calls
Access to off-site telephones is by dialing 9.
Long Distance Calling
JSC Policy is that long distance calls should be restricted
to the conduct of official business of such urgency that no
of communications will suffice. When
telephone calls to other cities are necessary, every effort
must be made to utilize the Federal Telecommunications
System (FTS).  Toll calls from non-Government
telephones must be made using Federal calling cards
provided by the Information Systems Directorate (ISD).
Access to FTS is by dialing 9+1.
Distance carriers such as Telecom*USA (10-10-220) are
blocked at JSC. Users are expected to dial their personai
credit card toll free number for long distance calls.
International Calling
International long distance calls originating from JSC on-
site telephones and from Ellington should be made by
dialing 9 + 011 + country code + city code + telephone
number.
Calls from non-Government phones should be made
using the Federal Calling cards provided by ISD.
Incoming Collect Calls
Incoming collect local and long distance calls are to be
discouraged and, if accepted, will require validation as to
necessity in the performance of official business.
Directory Assistance
The use of Directory Assistance is discouraged and the
information number is blocked at JSC. Whenever
possible, employees are asked to look up telephone
c 0 эnł

numbers in the appropriate telephone directory or use
other measures to determine the number being dialed.
Call Detail Reports (CDR)
Long distance call information is collected and provided to
management each month for validation. All long distance
calls to non-NASA numbers are listed in these reports.
Long distance calls dialed as shown below do NOT show
up in the CDR provided to each organization:
9 + 1 + 800 (toll free numbers)
9 +1 + 877 (toli free numbers)
9 + 1 + 888 (toll free numbers)
Federal Information Relay Service (FIRS)
1-800-877-8339
The FIRS relays calls between individuals who are deaf,
hard of hearing, or speech impaired, with individuals who
speak and hear. Both TDD/TTY and voice users may
initiate calls through the FIRS by dialing 1-800-877-8339.
The FIRS hours of operation are 8 a.m. through 8 p.m.,
Eastern Time, Monday through Friday, except for Federal
holidays.
Voice Mail
Voice Mail is a voice telephone messaging system
available at JSC.
Optiset Telephone Features
The Center Telecommunications System (CTS) provides
a variety of special features available to users based on a
Class of Service assignment. Please refer to the Optiset
Quick Reference Guide or User's Guide for procedures
relating to these features. Copies of Quick Reference and
User Guides can be obtained from your CTS Coordinator,
by calling the Help Desk at 483-4800, or by downloading
a version on the JSC Phone page.
CTS Coordinators
CTS Coordinators are designated by the Division Chief to
provide telecommunications support and are the single
point of contact between their organization and CTS.
CTS  Coordinators  can  be  found  at
http://www3.jsc.nasa.gov/phone/data/coords.doc.
Directory Distribution
The JSC Phone Directory is printed only on a limited
basis. The most current information can always be found
on the Directory Web site at http://www3.jsc.nasa.gov/
cgi-bin/prod/phone/phone.cgl. From that site, users may
print locally PDF versions of any and all sections of the
phone book as they are updated. If hard copies are
required in larger quantities, please submit a JSC Form
31 to the JSC Print Shop.
5

=== PDF PAGE 9 · Severe weather information ===
The annual hurricane season is from June 1
through November 30 with the peak period from
July through September. JSC has prepared detailed
plans in the event that a hurricane threatens this
immediate area. Each directorate, program office,
and contractor has designated a representative to
assist in the implementation of storm protection
procedures.
When it has been determined that a hurricane is threatening this area, JSC employees will be kept
informed of the Center's readiness status through the JSC television system, by recorded telephone
messages on extension 33351, and the toll-free Emergency Information Line (877-283-1947). Notice
of changes in work assignments and the possible closing of various Center facilities will be
disseminated by the media as well as through normal administrative channels.
All employees, prior to their departure from the Center, should secure their work areas to minimize
wind and water damage. Classitied information should be placed in proper storage; other important
materials should be protected as feasible; and electrical appliances and equipment should be
unplugged and stored as appropriate.
When a tornado threatens, take shelter in the interior of a building IMMEDIATELY. If shelter is not
available or there is no time to get indoors, lie in a ditch or low-lying area or crouch near a strong
building. Use arms to protect head and neck.
JSC employees are encouraged to be aware of the possibility of hurricanes and to make any
necessary personal protection preparations well in advance of a possible storm.

=== PDF PAGE 10 · Safety and health information ===
Safety and Health Information
If you see an unsafe act or condition take the following actions in
the order listed:
(keep going until you get the problem resolved)
1. STOP IT! Especially if there is imminent danger to life or health
2. Fix it yourself (if qualified and authorized) then submit a Close Call Report
3. Alert your Supervisor and submit a Close Call Report
4.  Report it to the Facility Manager and submit a Close Call Report
5. Submit a Close Call Report
6. Call the Safety Action Hotline 483-7500
7. Call the Center Director's Hotline 483-1234
8.  Submit an NSRS Report found in building lobbies
(NASA Safety Reporting System)
9. Contact NASA HQ:
Associate Administrator for Space Flight (Code M)
Associate Administrator for Safety & Mission Assurance (Code Q)
Chief of Staff, NASA Administrator's Office (Code A)
Associate Deputy Administrator (Code A)
NASA Administrator (Code A)
10.  Call OSHA
Visit the Safety and Total Health Web site at
http://www4.jsc.nasa.gov/safety/
Anaust 10 2001
